Hi, thank you so much for having me. Um... I guess I'm here because I've been having a little trouble with... well, just keeping up with normal things? It's actually kind of embarrassing to admit, to be honest.

Or I'll completely blank on showing up for appointments or following through on simple errands. I try really hard to stay on top of it, I promise, it's just... it's started costing me some real opportunities lately, so I figured I should finally come in and ask for help.

Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? I really don't want to sound like I'm just making excuses or not trying hard enough, because I really do try to focus.

Um... yeah, I guess I do, a little bit? But I really try my best to be polite and sit still, I promise.

Um... I guess it kind of affects a little bit of everything? But I really don't want to make it sound like my whole life is falling apart, because it's not! I'm really trying.

But, um... well, since I got let go from my last job—which, I mean, I know I need to take responsibility for—I've just been having a hard time keeping up. Like, at home, I'll completely miss following up on interviews, even when I really want the job. And socially... I actually had to step back from this volunteer project that I really, really cared about, just because I couldn't keep track of what I was supposed to be doing. 

So now I'm just sort of... unemployed, and I feel really bad about it. It just feels like I can't even manage the basic steps to get my life moving forward right now. I'm really sorry if that sounds pathetic. I'm usually much more capable than this, I promise. Does that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? I really try my best to follow instructions, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring people or being difficult on purpose. 

It's just... if someone gives me a set of steps to do—like for a job application, or a task for that volunteer thing I used to do, or even just cleaning my apartment—I always start out with really good intentions. I get really motivated! But then... I don't know, I just sort of... lose the thread? Like, I'll end up with all these half-finished forms, or emails I typed out but forgot to actually send. Or if I'm cleaning, I'll just have piles of things that I moved from one spot to another, but never actually put away. 

I feel so awful about it because I know it looks like I just don't care, or like I'm giving up halfway through, but I really am trying. I'm so sorry if that makes me sound completely unreliable. Is it... is it okay that I struggle with that? Do you see other people who do that too?

Um... I mean, I guess there are definitely times where I'm a little better? I really don't want to sound like I'm completely helpless every single day, because I'm not. I try really, really hard.

Or if I'm cleaning, I'll just have piles of things that I moved from one spot to another, but never actually put away. \n\nI feel so awful about it because I know it looks like I just don't care, or like I'm giving up halfway through, but I really am trying. I'm so sorry if that makes me sound completely unreliable. Is it... is it okay that I struggle with that? Do you see other people who do that too?\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you can focus and complete tasks without any trouble?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I guess there are definitely times where I'm a little better? I really don't want to sound like I'm completely helpless every single day, because I'm not. I try really, really hard. \n\nBut, um... honestly, if I think about it... I can't really remember the last time I had a whole day where everything just went smoothly from start to finish. Even on my \"good\" days, I feel like I'm using so much energy just trying to keep myself on track, and I still end up forgetting something small or getting distracted. It never feels completely easy. \n\nI'm really sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough to get better. I really want to be able to just do things normally. Does that... does that make sense? Is it bad that I don't really have totally clear days?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 22 / no_occupation / bachelors\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\nmanifestation: \"Quiet activities are hard for me because if I try to read, meditate, or sit calmly with friends, I end up tapping, humming, talking, or checking something instead of just being still and quiet.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I guess even with fun things, it can be a little tricky sometimes? I mean, I really love spending time with my friends, and I do enjoy reading and stuff. I don't want you to think I can't enjoy anything! 

But, um... if it's something really quiet... like if my friends just want to sit and chill, or if I try to read a book or do one of those meditation apps everyone says are so good for you... I just, I have a really hard time just... being still. Like, I'll realize I'm tapping my foot, or humming, or I just start talking because the quiet feels so loud? Or I'll just keep checking my phone instead of actually relaxing. 

I try so hard to just be calm and quiet like everyone else, but I always end up being the one fidgeting or making noise. I feel so bad because I don't want to annoy my friends or ruin the vibe when they're just trying to relax. I'm really sorry, I know I should be better at just sitting still when I'm supposed to. Is that... is it okay that I struggle with that even when I'm supposed to be having fun?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yeah, I guess even when things are super structured, it's still... kind of hard? I really try to be respectful, especially in important situations, I promise. I don't want to seem rude or like I'm not paying attention.

But, like... if I have to sit through something really formal, like an interview, or a meeting, or honestly even just sitting in a movie theater... I get this really intense feeling. It's like this... this pressure building up inside my body? Like I just absolutely *have* to get up, or shift in my seat, or do something with my hands. If I try to just sit perfectly still, it almost feels like I'm going to crawl out of my skin. 

I try really, really hard to hide it, because I know you're supposed to just sit quietly and be polite during those things. I'll just, like, squeeze my hands together under the table so no one sees. I'm so sorry, I know it probably looks really unprofessional when I can't just sit normally. Do you think... is it bad that I feel that way even when I know I'm supposed to be on my best behavior?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yeah, I think so. I really tried to be a good student back then, I promise I wasn't trying to be difficult or a troublemaker! But... yeah, even back in elementary school, it was kind of an issue. 

My teachers were really sweet, they would always write on my report cards that I was bright, which I really appreciated. But they also... well, they noted that I was constantly losing my worksheets. And I guess I had a bad habit of rushing through directions instead of reading them carefully. I also talked out of turn a lot... which I feel so terrible about now, because I know how disrespectful that is to the teacher and the other kids. 

And, oh gosh, my desk... I'm so embarrassed to admit this, but my desk was always so messy that I literally couldn't find my assignments when it was time to turn them in. I really did want to be organized, it just somehow always ended up a complete disaster. 

I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds like I was a really careless kid to have in class. Does that... does that answer your question okay? Is that the kind of thing you needed to know?

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8, F98.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, thank you so much for having me. Um... I guess I'm here because I've been having a little trouble with... well, just keeping up with normal things? It's actually kind of embarrassing to admit, to be honest. \n\nI just... I keep forgetting basic adult things. Like, I'll forget to pay my bills, or... I won't return emails for job interviews, which is really bad since I just graduated. Or I'll completely blank on showing up for appointments or following through on simple errands. I try really hard to stay on top of it, I promise, it's just... it's started costing me some real opportunities lately, so I figured I should finally come in and ask for help. \n\nUm, does that make sense? Is that okay to start with?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you think about all the things you're trying to keep up with?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, maybe a little? But I really don't want to sound dramatic or anything. I wouldn't say it's like, real anxiety. \n\nI just... I guess I get a little stressed sometimes when I realize how much I've let pile up. But I think everyone gets a little overwhelmed trying to figure things out after graduating, right? I really try to stay positive about it and just promise myself I'll do better the next day. I definitely don't want you to think I'm just sitting around crying or freaking out all the time. I'm actually usually a pretty upbeat person! \n\nUm... is that the kind of answer you were looking for? I hope I'm explaining this right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to tasks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? I really don't want to sound like I'm just making excuses or not trying hard enough, because I really do try to focus. \n\nBut, like... I'll sit down at my laptop to update my resume, or maybe work on a coding practice problem, and... I don't know. A text notification will pop up on my phone, or I'll hear someone talking outside my window, or honestly, even just a random thought about another task will pop into my head. And then, before I even notice what happened, I'm completely off track and doing something entirely different. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound lazy. I always feel so guilty when it happens. Is... is that normal? Do a lot of people struggle with that when they're looking for jobs?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do, a little bit? But I really try my best to be polite and sit still, I promise. \n\nIt's just... if I have to sit through something long, like an interview or a meeting, or even just watching a movie with friends... I get this weird, uncomfortable feeling. It's almost like this pressure building up inside my body? Like I just really, really need to get up and move around, or shift in my seat, or at least do something with my hands. If I don't, I start feeling like I'm going to crawl right out of my skin. \n\nI try really hard to hide it, especially in professional settings, so I don't look rude or bored. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad listener or anything. Is that... does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and forgetfulness at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it kind of affects a little bit of everything? But I really don't want to make it sound like my whole life is falling apart, because it's not! I'm really trying.\n\nBut, um... well, since I got let go from my last job—which, I mean, I know I need to take responsibility for—I've just been having a hard time keeping up. Like, at home, I'll completely miss following up on interviews, even when I really want the job. And socially... I actually had to step back from this volunteer project that I really, really cared about, just because I couldn't keep track of what I was supposed to be doing. \n\nSo now I'm just sort of... unemployed, and I feel really bad about it. It just feels like I can't even manage the basic steps to get my life moving forward right now. I'm really sorry if that sounds pathetic. I'm usually much more capable than this, I promise. Does that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with focus and following through on tasks in all parts of your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? I really try my best to follow instructions, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring people or being difficult on purpose. \n\nIt's just... if someone gives me a set of steps to do—like for a job application, or a task for that volunteer thing I used to do, or even just cleaning my apartment—I always start out with really good intentions. I get really motivated! But then... I don't know, I just sort of... lose the thread? Like, I'll end up with all these half-finished forms, or emails I typed out but forgot to actually send. Or if I'm cleaning, I'll just have piles of things that I moved from one spot to another, but never actually put away. \n\nI feel so awful about it because I know it looks like I just don't care, or like I'm giving up halfway through, but I really am trying. I'm so sorry if that makes me sound completely unreliable. Is it... is it okay that I struggle with that? Do you see other people who do that too?\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you can focus and complete tasks without any trouble?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I guess there are definitely times where I'm a little better? I really don't want to sound like I'm completely helpless every single day, because I'm not. I try really, really hard. \n\nBut, um... honestly, if I think about it... I can't really remember the last time I had a whole day where everything just went smoothly from start to finish. Even on my \"good\" days, I feel like I'm using so much energy just trying to keep myself on track, and I still end up forgetting something small or getting distracted. It never feels completely easy. \n\nI'm really sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough to get better. I really want to be able to just do things normally. Does that... does that make sense? Is it bad that I don't really have totally clear days?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess even with fun things, it can be a little tricky sometimes? I mean, I really love spending time with my friends, and I do enjoy reading and stuff. I don't want you to think I can't enjoy anything! \n\nBut, um... if it's something really quiet... like if my friends just want to sit and chill, or if I try to read a book or do one of those meditation apps everyone says are so good for you... I just, I have a really hard time just... being still. Like, I'll realize I'm tapping my foot, or humming, or I just start talking because the quiet feels so loud? Or I'll just keep checking my phone instead of actually relaxing. \n\nI try so hard to just be calm and quiet like everyone else, but I always end up being the one fidgeting or making noise. I feel so bad because I don't want to annoy my friends or ruin the vibe when they're just trying to relax. I'm really sorry, I know I should be better at just sitting still when I'm supposed to. Is that... is it okay that I struggle with that even when I'm supposed to be having fun?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness even when you're doing things that are highly structured or routine?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess even when things are super structured, it's still... kind of hard? I really try to be respectful, especially in important situations, I promise. I don't want to seem rude or like I'm not paying attention.\n\nBut, like... if I have to sit through something really formal, like an interview, or a meeting, or honestly even just sitting in a movie theater... I get this really intense feeling. It's like this... this pressure building up inside my body? Like I just absolutely *have* to get up, or shift in my seat, or do something with my hands. If I try to just sit perfectly still, it almost feels like I'm going to crawl out of my skin. \n\nI try really, really hard to hide it, because I know you're supposed to just sit quietly and be polite during those things. I'll just, like, squeeze my hands together under the table so no one sees. I'm so sorry, I know it probably looks really unprofessional when I can't just sit normally. Do you think... is it bad that I feel that way even when I know I'm supposed to be on my best behavior?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness at home, at work, or in social situations consistently, or are there specific places or activities where they are more or less pronounced?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it kind of happens everywhere? I really try to keep it under control, especially when I'm around other people, because I don't want to be a bother. \n\nBut, um... whether I'm at home trying to clean, or if I'm out with friends, or... well, I'm not working right now, but when I was in school, it was definitely there too. I don't think there's really a specific place where it completely goes away. I mean, maybe if I'm doing something really, really active it's a little less noticeable? But even then, my mind is usually still racing. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know it would probably be easier to figure out if it was just happening in one place. I try so hard to be present and focused no matter where I am. I hope that's an okay answer... does that help at all, or did you need me to be more specific?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations where you feel your symptoms of inattention and restlessness are completely absent?\n\nPatient: Um... completely absent? I... I don't think so? I really wish I could say yes, because I know it would probably make things simpler. I mean, I try really hard to find things that make me feel totally focused and calm, I promise I do. \n\nBut, um... even when I'm doing something I really love, or when I'm trying my absolute best to just pay attention... there's always at least a little bit of that restless feeling, or my mind will just sort of... drift off for a second. I try to catch it right away so nobody notices! But... yeah, I don't think there's anything where it's just completely, one hundred percent gone. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds like I'm not trying hard enough to manage it. Is it really bad that I can't find anything that makes it stop completely? I hope I'm not making this too difficult for you to figure out...\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness when you were a child, before you turned 12?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think so. I really tried to be a good student back then, I promise I wasn't trying to be difficult or a troublemaker! But... yeah, even back in elementary school, it was kind of an issue. \n\nMy teachers were really sweet, they would always write on my report cards that I was bright, which I really appreciated. But they also... well, they noted that I was constantly losing my worksheets. And I guess I had a bad habit of rushing through directions instead of reading them carefully. I also talked out of turn a lot... which I feel so terrible about now, because I know how disrespectful that is to the teacher and the other kids. \n\nAnd, oh gosh, my desk... I'm so embarrassed to admit this, but my desk was always so messy that I literally couldn't find my assignments when it was time to turn them in. I really did want to be organized, it just somehow always ended up a complete disaster. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds like I was a really careless kid to have in class. Does that... does that answer your question okay? Is that the kind of thing you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with focus and restlessness consistently throughout your childhood and into adulthood?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess the things from when I was a kid were always kind of there in the background, but... I mean, I really tried to manage them, and I thought I was doing an okay job for a long time. \n\nBut as for it being this bad... um, no, it hasn't been like this my whole life. I really don't want you to think I've just been letting things slide forever! It's really only been, um... about a year and two months that it's been seriously affecting me like this. \n\nIt kind of started getting really hard to manage right around the time things started falling apart at my old job... I was working at a biotech company, and I tried so, so hard to keep up, but I just... couldn't. And then, well, after I was let go, the difficulties just sort of continued and got worse. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know it's probably confusing that it was somewhat okay and then got so much worse recently. I hope it's alright that the timeline is a little messy like that... is that helpful for you to know?\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with focus and restlessness been present for more than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, definitely more than six months. I know I mentioned it just a second ago, but it's been about a year and two months since it got really, really hard to handle. \n\nI feel so bad repeating myself, I hope I'm not wasting your time! But yeah, it was right around the time I started struggling so much at the biotech company, and then... well, it just hasn't gotten any better since I was let go. I've really been trying to get back on track since then, I promise I have. I hope that makes sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I guess they have, maybe a little bit. I really try not to let it affect other people, I promise I do! But... um, since getting fired, things have been kind of... messy. 

I feel terrible even admitting this, because I know I should be doing better, but I've actually missed a few follow-ups for new job interviews. I just completely lose track, even when I'm trying so hard to stay on top of it and be professional. And... well, I also had to step back from a volunteer project that I really, really cared about, just because I couldn't keep up with what I was supposed to be doing. 

So, um, I'm still unemployed right now, and I honestly just feel like I can't reliably manage the basic steps of moving my life forward. I'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful and I don't want you to think I'm just giving up, because I really am trying to fix it. Is that... does that answer what you were asking?

I'm so sorry if that makes me sound completely unreliable. Is it... is it okay that I struggle with that? Do you see other people who do that too?\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you can focus and complete tasks without any trouble?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I guess there are definitely times where I'm a little better? I really don't want to sound like I'm completely helpless every single day, because I'm not. I try really, really hard. \n\nBut, um... honestly, if I think about it... I can't really remember the last time I had a whole day where everything just went smoothly from start to finish. Even on my \"good\" days, I feel like I'm using so much energy just trying to keep myself on track, and I still end up forgetting something small or getting distracted. It never feels completely easy. \n\nI'm really sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough to get better. I really want to be able to just do things normally. Is that... is it okay that I struggle with that even when I'm supposed to be having fun?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness even when you're doing things that are highly structured or routine?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess even when things are super structured, it's still... kind of hard? I really try to be respectful, especially in important situations, I promise. I don't want to seem rude or like I'm not paying attention.\n\nBut, like... if I have to sit through something really formal, like an interview, or a meeting, or honestly even just sitting in a movie theater... I get this really intense feeling. It's like this... this pressure building up inside my body? Like I just absolutely *have* to get up, or shift in my seat, or do something with my hands. If I try to just sit perfectly still, it almost feels like I'm going to crawl out of my skin. \n\nI try really, really hard to hide it, because I know you're supposed to just sit quietly and be polite during those things. I'll just, like, squeeze my hands together under the table so no one sees. I'm so sorry, I know it probably looks really unprofessional when I can't just sit normally. Do you think... is it bad that I feel that way even when I know I'm supposed to be on my best behavior?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness at home, at work, or in social situations consistently, or are there specific places or activities where they are more or less pronounced?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it kind of happens everywhere? I really try to keep it under control, especially when I'm around other people, because I don't want to be a bother. \n\nBut, um... whether I'm at home trying to clean, or if I'm out with friends, or... well, I'm not working right now, but when I was in school, it was definitely there too. I don't think there's really a specific place where it completely goes away. I mean, maybe if I'm doing something really, really active it's a little less noticeable? But even then, my mind is usually still racing. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know it would probably be easier to figure out if it was just happening in one place. I try so hard to be present and focused no matter where I am. I hope that's an okay answer... does that help at all, or did you need me to be more specific?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations where you feel your symptoms of inattention and restlessness are completely absent?\n\nPatient: Um... completely absent? I... I don't think so? I really wish I could say yes, because I know it would probably make things simpler. I mean, I try really hard to find things that make me feel totally focused and calm, I promise I do. \n\nBut, um... even when I'm doing something I really love, or when I'm trying my absolute best to just pay attention... there's always at least a little bit of that restless feeling, or my mind will just sort of... drift off for a second. I try to catch it right away so nobody notices! But... yeah, I don't think there's anything where it's just completely, one hundred percent gone. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds like I'm not trying hard enough to manage it. Is it really bad that I can't find anything that makes it stop completely? I hope I'm not making this too difficult for you to figure out...\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness when you were a child, before you turned 12?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think so. I really tried to be a good student back then, I promise I wasn't trying to be difficult or a troublemaker! But... yeah, even back in elementary school, it was kind of an issue. \n\nMy teachers were really sweet, they would always write on my report cards that I was bright, which I really appreciated. I really did want to be organized, it just somehow always ended up a complete disaster. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds like I was a really careless kid to have in class. Does that... does that answer your question okay? Is that the kind of thing you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with focus and restlessness consistently throughout your childhood and into adulthood?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess the things from when I was a kid were always kind of there in the background, but... I mean, I really tried to manage them, and I thought I was doing an okay job for a long time. \n\nBut as for it being this bad... um, no, it hasn't been like this my whole life. I really don't want you to think I've just been letting things slide forever! It's really only been, um... about a year and two months that it's been seriously affecting me like this. \n\nIt kind of started getting really hard to manage right around the time things started falling apart at my old job... I was working at a biotech company, and I tried so, so hard to keep up, but I just... couldn't. And then, well, after I was let go, the difficulties just sort of continued and got worse. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know it's probably confusing that it was somewhat okay and then got so much worse recently. I hope it's alright that the timeline is a little messy like that... is that helpful for you to know?\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with focus and restlessness been present for more than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, definitely more than six months. I try so hard to be present and focused no matter where I am. I... I don't think so? I really wish I could say yes, because I know it would probably make things simpler. Um... well, I think it's kind of always been there, to be honest. I really hate admitting this, because I always wanted to be a good student, and I swear I really tried to be! So, yeah, I guess I've struggled with these things since I was little, even though I've always tried really hard to fix it. Does that... is that the kind of background information you needed? I hope I'm not oversharing or anything...

If I don't, I start feeling like I'm going to crawl right out of my skin. \n\nI try really hard to hide it, especially in professional settings, so I don't look rude or bored. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad listener or anything. Is that... does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and forgetfulness at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Um... Um... yeah, they have been. I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining, because I know I should be able to handle things better, but... it's been pretty consistently bad for a while now.

Is that... is it okay that I struggle with that even when I'm supposed to be having fun?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness even when you're doing things that are highly structured or routine?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess even when things are super structured, it's still... kind of hard? I really try to be respectful, especially in important situations, I promise. I don't want to seem rude or like I'm not paying attention.\n\nBut, like... if I have to sit through something really formal, like an interview, or a meeting, or honestly even just sitting in a movie theater... I get this really intense feeling. It's like this... this pressure building up inside my body? Like I just absolutely *have* to get up, or shift in my seat, or do something with my hands. If I try to just sit perfectly still, it almost feels like I'm going to crawl out of my skin. \n\nI try really, really hard to hide it, because I know you're supposed to just sit quietly and be polite during those things. I'll just, like, squeeze my hands together under the table so no one sees. I'm so sorry, I know it probably looks really unprofessional when I can't just sit normally. Do you think... is it bad that I feel that way even when I know I'm supposed to be on my best behavior?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness at home, at work, or in social situations consistently, or are there specific places or activities where they are more or less pronounced?\n\nPatient: Um... I really don't want to sound like I'm just making excuses or not trying hard enough, because I really do try to focus. \n\nBut, like... I'll sit down at my laptop to update my resume, or maybe work on a coding practice problem, and... I don't know. A text notification will pop up on my phone, or I'll hear someone talking outside my window, or honestly, even just a random thought about another task will pop into my head. And then, before I even notice what happened, I'm completely off track and doing something entirely different. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound lazy. I always feel so guilty when it happens. Is... is that normal? Do a lot of people struggle with that when they're looking for jobs?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do, a little bit? But I really try my best to be polite and sit still, I promise. \n\nIt's just... if I have to sit through something long, like an interview or a meeting, or even just watching a movie with friends... I get this weird, uncomfortable feeling. It's almost like this pressure building up inside my body? Like I just really, really need to get up and move around, or shift in my seat, or at least do something with my hands. If I don't, I start feeling like I'm going to crawl right out of my skin. \n\nI try really hard to hide it, especially in professional settings, so I don't look rude or bored. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad listener or anything. Is that... does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and forgetfulness at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it kind of affects a little bit of everything? But I really don't want to make it sound like my whole life is falling apart, because it's not! I'm really trying.\n\nBut, um... well, since I got let go from my last job—which, I mean, I know I need to take responsibility for—I've just been having a hard time keeping up. Like, at home, I'll completely miss following up on interviews, even when I really want the job. And socially... I actually had to step back from this volunteer project that I really, really cared about, just because I couldn't keep track of what I was supposed to be doing. \n\nSo now I'm just sort of... unemployed, and I feel really bad about it. It just feels like I can't even manage the basic steps to get my life moving forward right now. I'm really sorry if that sounds pathetic. I'm usually much more capable than this, I promise. Does that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with focus and following through on tasks in all parts of your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? I really try my best to follow instructions, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring people or being difficult on purpose. \n\nIt's just... if someone gives me a set of steps to do—like for a job application, or a task for that volunteer thing I used to do, or even just cleaning my apartment—I always start out with really good intentions. I get really motivated! But then... I don't know, I just sort of... lose the thread? Like, I'll end up with all these half-finished forms, or emails I typed out but forgot to actually send. Or if I'm cleaning, I'll just have piles of things that I moved from one spot to another, but never actually put away. \n\nI feel so awful about it because I know it looks like I just don't care, or like I'm giving up halfway through, but I really am trying. I'm so sorry if that makes me sound completely unreliable. Is it... is it okay that I struggle with that? Do you see other people who do that too?\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you can focus and complete tasks without any trouble?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I guess there are definitely times where I'm a little better? I really don't want to sound like I'm completely helpless every single day, because I'm not. I try really, really hard. \n\nBut, um... honestly, if I think about it... I can't really remember the last time I had a whole day where everything just went smoothly from start to finish. Even on my \"good\" days, I feel like I'm using so much energy just trying to keep myself on track, and I still end up forgetting something small or getting distracted. It never feels completely easy. \n\nI'm really sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough to get better. I really want to be able to just do things normally. I really try to be respectful, especially in important situations, I promise. I don't want to seem rude or like I'm not paying attention.\n\nBut, like... if I have to sit through something really formal, like an interview, or a meeting, or honestly even just sitting in a movie theater... I get this really intense feeling. It's like this... this pressure building up inside my body? Like I just absolutely *have* to get up, or shift in my seat, or do something with my hands. But even then, my mind is usually still racing. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know it would probably be easier to figure out if it was just happening in one place. I mean, I try really hard to find things that make me feel totally focused and calm, I promise I do. \n\nBut, um... even when I'm doing something I really love, or when I'm trying my absolute best to just pay attention... there's always at least a little bit of that restless feeling, or my mind will just sort of... drift off for a second. I hope I'm not making this too difficult for you to figure out...\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these difficulties with focus and restlessness when you were a child, before you turned 12?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think so.
